# Musicality development progress

This is the chronological engineering and listening handoff for the `musicality` branch. The [implementation ledger](/musicality) remains the requirement-by-requirement record; the [architecture roadmap](/docs/architecture/musicality) explains the design. Passing tests establishes behavior, not musical excellence or human listening acceptance.

## Overnight working agreement — 24–25 October 2026

Continue coherent musicality milestones overnight, document progress and retain clear checkpoints. Keep all work on `musicality`; leave `dev` and production available for their existing purposes. Use local and sidecar validation, `[skip ci]` commits, non-force pushes and exact remote SHA verification. Do not run hosted CircleCI or release experimental accompaniment during this work.

Preserve the musical character of Swing, Straight, Bossa Nova and Ballad. Original mode remains the compatibility baseline. Keep causal observations, immutable committed events, deterministic replay, checkpoint identity, protected pulse and explicit form semantics. Do not weaken existing tests to hide regressions. Fix a blocker or document it and continue independent useful work. Human listening remains pending until a person records judgments over complete tunes.

Overnight continuation is scheduled in the existing chat every 15 minutes through 08:00 America/Los_Angeles on 25 October 2026. Local execution requires the computer and app to remain running. Each milestone below records what changed, validation, limitations, listening guidance and a commit checkpoint. No raw microphone audio or private reasoning belongs in this log.

## Starting checkpoint — `98a59f26`

Structural performance energy is implemented for explicit coordinated 4/4 forms. Heads are restrained, solo choruses build and release, returning heads ease back, and the final closing phrase releases energy. The same policy drives fixed and rolling generation, with a compact audio-clock **Planned arc** cue on `/musicality`.

Validation: 6,457 tests passed locally and on the sidecar, with 40 existing expected failures; 45 browser checks passed across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; typecheck/build passed. Five discovered regressions were fixed without weakening their assertions: earlier loop prefixes remain stable when future loops are added, and nonterminal releases retain eligible bass launches and drum setups. Final release follows the actual final section's closing phrase, including irregular lengths. Commit `98a59f26946eb34318e4dcf6d0940b5ada86c142` was verified on `origin/musicality`.

Remaining: deliberate texture transitions, reciprocal dialogue beyond recent-piano drum answers, richer voicing objectives, acoustic validation and recorded human acceptance. Production integration remains separate because the current release base has newer playback/form fixes.

## 25 October — deliberate structural textures

Intent: make the arrangement's texture audible through sustained player choices, rather than relying only on energy weights.

- Bass: explicit head/solo-aware two-feel and walking choices, including return to the melody, shared by fixed and rolling generation. Preserve eligible transitions and already committed launches.
- Drums: selected sustained ride-bell color at appropriate solo peaks, latched to a phrase, with a return to ordinary ride and protected quarter-note pulse. Preserve native Bossa and Ballad grooves.
- Piano: inspect deliberate voicing expansion and line objectives before selecting the next implementation; do not claim work completed from an audit alone.

Implemented in explicit Coordinated Swing/Straight forms. Heads and returning heads use two-feel; solo choruses walk. A seeded structural choice can walk the last two bars into a declared solo, and quiet final phrases can return to two. Bass durations stop at intervening harmonic changes; already committed launches and short solo arrivals remain intact. Selected complete solo-peak phrases sustain bell color with a cooldown, then return to ride. Original and native Bossa/Ballad paths remain unchanged. The live musician view exposes planned feel and cymbal color alongside actual emitted attacks, including honest silence states.

Behavioral tests first reproduced the missing returning-head two-feel, missing sustained bell time, misleading presenter action and overlapping bass release at an odd-beat harmonic change. The fixes passed those tests without changing existing assertions. An independent full-tune Saints test verifies actual bell time, fixed/rolling decisions, JSON restoration within a bell phrase and the final return to two-feel. Review found the harmonic-release defect; it is fixed and covered. Typechecking also caught a readonly test-helper annotation, which was corrected.

Local and sidecar unit validation: **6,477 passed, 40 expected failures**, across 414 files. Focused instrument partitions passed 95 bass and 71 drum/ensemble/rolling/style cases; presenter tests passed 15 cases; the independent real-tune matrix passed both styles. Ledger/docs checks passed 56 cases. Typechecking passed with zero errors/warnings; production build passed. All **45 browser checks passed** across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, including complete-tune playback, matched blind reviews, native styles, cancellation and the new progress-log navigation. Hosted CircleCI was not run. This entry ships in the structural-texture checkpoint; the next entry records its verified commit SHA.

Audition: on `/musicality`, use **When the Saints Go Marching In**, Coordinated Swing or Straight, with four solo choruses. Listen to the head's half-note pulse, walking entry into the solo, selected later solo-peak bell phrase, then two-feel under the returning melody. Bell selection is seeded and deliberately occasional. A 12-bar Blues peak can be too short or misaligned for the existing phrase, so it retains ordinary ride. Compare complete takes at matched settings; record observations in the listening marks or blind review. Human acceptance remains pending.

Next: shared causal piano voicing placement based on the last chord actually emitted. Preserve existing family choices and native style biases; address legal defining tones, common-tone retention and guide-tone continuity with actual musical examples. Structural piano texture expansion follows as a separate increment. Production rollout, richer reciprocal dialogue, bass sample-layer correction/balance and acoustic validation remain separate work.

## 25 October — causal piano voice-leading

Structural textures were committed and verified on `origin/musicality` as `c79f4d262e3a43cf7d9dd70baaa6045b0c02e4ff`. That milestone took approximately ten minutes of overlapping work: reading/planning about two minutes, implementation five, and review/documentation three. Measured local unit tests took 23.54 seconds, sidecar unit tests 68.11 seconds (81 seconds including sync), and the slowest check was the browser matrix at 3.5 minutes. These estimates overlap and must not be added as serial elapsed time.

The next increment is deliberately limited to explicit-form Coordinated piano placement. Fixed playback currently voice-leads through harmony even when the piano rests; rolling playback follows emitted voicings. A shared causal resolver will make the previous **sounding** chord the reference, preserve existing family choices and native style biases, and test guide-tone motion/common-tone retention using concrete ii–V–I examples. Original and metadata-free compatibility paths remain unchanged. New structural piano texture thresholds are deferred to a separate milestone.

Implemented: a bounded resolver chooses legal inversions from the selected family using the last actual chord, with defining-tone motion, exact common tones, total movement and register objectives. The Dm7 F3/C4 → G7 F3/B3 → Cmaj7 E3/B3 example works in all twelve keys. Coordinated guide/shell shapes identify the major third of a sharp-nine dominant correctly; altered drop-2 shapes retain the seventh and named color tone. Quartal shapes retain fourth stacks and do not claim a guide-tone objective. The current attack bar supplies energy even when the chord anticipates upcoming harmony. Only emitted attacks advance memory or create evidence; checkpoints validate that memory and bind the new policy.

Behavioral reds reproduced the missing ii–V–I connection, sharp-nine guide-tone error, influence from unheard intermediate harmony, memory updates after rejected hits and future-energy leakage into an anticipation. These are fixed without weakening existing assertions. The musician view now shows matched actual voicing families, common tones and guide-motion evidence; absent attacks, mismatched notes and Original mode cannot acquire invented explanations.

Validation: **6,500 tests passed locally and on the sidecar**, with 40 expected failures across 417 files. Focused resolver/legacy voicing checks passed 54 cases; causal integration/ensemble/rolling/style/goldens passed 65; independent musical/native-style integration passed five; presenter tests passed 17. Typechecking passed with zero errors/warnings and the build passed. All **45 browser checks passed** across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, including full-tune exported voicing evidence, blind reviews and lifecycle behavior. No hosted CircleCI ran. This entry ships in the causal-piano checkpoint; the following entry records its verified commit SHA.

Independent Saints evidence at identical rhythmic inputs: attack counts, times, durations, velocities and motif traces stayed identical in all four styles. Largest chord-center leaps decreased from 7.25 to 4.67 semitones in Swing, 11.5 to 5.75 in Straight, 10.25 to 6.92 in Bossa and 12 to 4.75 in Ballad. This is one scenario, not an acceptance threshold. A separate local generation benchmark across both tunes/all styles measured 4.95–22.02 ms for a complete fixed take; rolling bar p95 was 0.59–0.84 ms and the maximum 2.13 ms. Realized candidate counts were 2–36. These are single-run measurements, not a latency guarantee.

Audition: compare complete Coordinated takes with the previous checkpoint, focusing on the piano’s connections after rests, ii–V–I changes and altered dominants. Native Bossa and Ballad rhythms should remain recognizable. Repeated harmony intentionally favors stable common tones over following a rising register target; structural voicing expansion and a composed top-note line remain future work. Human listening acceptance is pending.

Next investigation: repair source-defined Smolken bass velocity/round-robin selection, which currently layers 7–12 recordings for one note. Keep the repair bank-specific, preserve current gain initially, verify actual native source counts, and retain same-gain before/after full-tune audio measurements before judging balance.

## Source-defined bass sampling and measured balance — 25 October 2026

Previous checkpoint: `c6f8043d289f65bb32bf7c28fe710955bb786a91` (causal piano voice-leading), pushed and verified on `origin/musicality`.

The installed Smolken converter loses one-sided velocity bounds and region round-robin lengths. A single bass note consequently starts 7–12 recordings. The bank-specific repair restores source velocity bands and three/four-recording cycles for MIDI 28–55, with one recording selected per note. It applies to Original and Coordinated in every style, through both live and offline sample loading. Unknown or modified banks are left intact. The complete source SFZ is retained as an independent test fixture; unsupported source velocity curves remain a separate limitation.

Native Chromium, Firefox and WebKit checks each exercised 1,352 actual note triggers: exactly one intended recording, correct layer/cycle, finite PCM and direct-sampler attacks within 0–6 ms. The selection-only revision passed 6,527 tests locally and on the sidecar, with 40 expected failures, plus typecheck/build.

Same-event, same-gain 64-bar Saints bass stems in all four styles exposed a 17.2–19.2 dB RMS drop when accidental layering was removed. Raw before/after WAVs, event inputs, source hashes, methods and public-app full mixes are retained in `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-25/bass-selection/listen.html`. These are local engineering artifacts. Public WAV export peak-normalizes its output, so a normalized solo bounce cannot establish relative ensemble balance; raw pre-normalization measurements are retained separately.

A measured bank-qualified linear compensation now loads through the same adapter in live and offline playback. Its factor 7.74 is the rounded geometric mean of the four before/after RMS ratios, restoring this reference set within about 1.4 dB. The global bass trim stays 0.05; unknown banks receive no compensation. This is reference-level preservation, not a claim of ideal musical balance. Human full-tune listening acceptance remains pending.

Six native sample-selection/calibration browser checks passed in 28.6 seconds across all three engines: actual RMS/peak gain is 7.74 for the recognized bank and 1.0 for an intentionally modified bank. Full PCM differs only by the expected scalar (maximum sample error below 1.8e-8), source scheduling is unchanged, exactly one gain is inserted, and repeated instrument disconnection remains safe. The empirical reference fixture records source and event checksums; it protects the four-style 1.5 dB reference-level bound.

Review caught and fixed a partial-insert cleanup edge case: disconnect the instrument’s owned chain before the gain, and preserve the original failure if cleanup also throws. One earlier readiness test assumed bass and piano use the identical loader object; it now checks their deliberate wrapper separation while retaining readiness/disposal assertions. A local full-suite run under concurrent native rendering included two unrelated integration tests exceeding their existing five-second timeouts; no timeout or assertion was relaxed, and the bounded-worker full rerun passed all 6,541 tests plus 40 expected failures (30.55 seconds, four workers). The sidecar final suite passed 6,541 tests plus 40 expected failures (62.98 seconds). Typecheck is clean; production build completed in 6.01 seconds.

Calibrated public-app validation retained eight raw pre-normalization profiles (full mix and isolated bass for every style) and four complete 44.1 kHz stereo WAVs at `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-25/bass-calibration/listen.html`. Actual isolated-bass RMS gain is 7.73999977 in all four styles. Full-mix peaks are 0.251559 Swing, 0.250790 Straight, 0.204066 Bossa and 0.061419 Ballad, leaving at least 11.99 dB raw headroom in this dry reference setup. These results do not guarantee every possible mix setting or establish perceptual balance. The earlier uncalibrated raw artifacts remain unchanged.

Public-app browser validation passed 28 checks with two existing Firefox real-render skips in 5.5 minutes; all three engines passed the real bass/piano attack check. Final snapshot: `e4e38534-ac07-4937-9da9-4f775674ea3f`. Pre-commit elapsed time was about 17 minutes with overlapping stages (reading approximately 2 minutes, implementation 4 minutes, review/documentation 4 minutes); the browser run was the slowest check. No hosted CircleCI ran.

Next bounded milestone: align explicit-form rolling piano family selection with the existing fixed policy. Fixed already scales shell/quartal preference by the accepted attack’s current energy; rolling currently omits that factor. Preserve fixed outputs, native style biases, Original/metadata-free behavior, causal history, attack timing and silence budgets, and version the explicit rolling checkpoint fingerprint. This is consistency repair, not new structural voicing expansion or solo-aware texture restraint. Deliberate three-note rootless support and qualified dense-solo voicing restraint remain subsequent work.

## Piano family policy parity — 25 October 2026

Previous checkpoint: `1cc7888f60a318c8cd980c1d5bc04b43d52bbcbe` (bass source selection and measured balance), pushed and verified on `origin/musicality`.

Existing Original/metadata-free code paths remain intact.

Baseline capture before edits retained 72 fixed and 48 rolling full-tune scenarios across Saints/Blues, C/E-flat/F-sharp and all four styles, with event and non-pitch hashes (`/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-25/piano-family-parity/baseline.json`, SHA-256 `e1433383945391f2270db4e82d69b126c9feaed647575585881feb447274e0ed`). The explicit rolling fingerprint advances to policy version 2; old-policy checkpoint rejection must be deliberate because remembered voicings influence later choices.

This consistency repair does not add structural voicing expansion or guarantee solo-aware chord thinning. Human listening remains pending.

The meaningful red regression is now green. Sixteen of the 24 explicit rolling scenarios changed pitches. Eight additional integration tests passed locally in 1.25 seconds, exercising actual native attack patterns across 32 seeds per style and qualified dense-solo silence without advancing emitted history. The final sidecar suite passed 6,553 tests plus 40 expected failures (52.45 seconds); typecheck passed with zero errors/warnings. Snapshot: `98179745-c59a-41a9-a019-02f0b01c5b53`. Independent review found no remaining issue.

Next planned texture increment: implement P20’s three-note rootless vocabulary for ordinary major-seventh, minor-seventh and dominant-seventh chords only. Let explicit form request support through heads/releases/endings and preserve existing full voicings through solo build/peak; qualified active-solo evidence must override toward support even on a permitted boundary attack. Keep unsupported chord/family vocabulary intact, add no attacks, preserve native style patterns and report requested versus actually applied texture. Version both direct ensemble and rolling checkpoint policies. This is planned work, not an implemented or listening-accepted claim.

## Three-note rootless support — 25 October 2026

Previous checkpoint: `766cec48b1bf26fc57ecb827b9ae90f813ecb98e` (shared causal piano family policy), pushed and verified on `origin/musicality`.

P20 now has a scoped implementation: selected rootless A/B on common major-seventh, minor-seventh and dominant-seventh harmony can use three notes. The ensemble requests support through heads/releases/endings, preserves the existing family shape through solo build/peak and prioritizes qualified active-solo evidence even on a permitted boundary attack. Unknown evidence remains a structural request, never a claimed soloist rest. Unsupported chords/families retain their vocabulary; there are no additional attacks.

The resolver constructs defining third/seventh plus ninth (dominant B: thirteenth) before causal placement. Optional emitted evidence distinguishes request, actual application, eligibility and note count. The musician view’s compact details preserve planned-versus-played distinctions and old-export compatibility. Both direct ensemble and rolling checkpoint policies are version-bound. Validation and common-gain full-tune listening evidence are recorded below; human musical acceptance remains pending.

Focused validation passed 39 engine/planner/integration tests across six files and 37 presenter tests. A captured prior direct-ensemble fingerprint from checkpoint `766cec48` is rejected by the new explicit policy while its metadata-free fingerprint remains unchanged. Tests cover qualified active evidence, stale/low-confidence/future/invalid observations, a permitted dense-solo boundary attack, nested immutability and JSON/window equivalence.

Independent comparison of 72 fixed and 48 rolling scenarios preserves all attack times, durations, velocities, counts, bass and drums. All 48 fixed Original-or-metadata-free scenarios and all 24 metadata-free rolling scenarios remain entirely identical; 23/24 explicit scenarios in each engine change pitches. Reference artifacts are retained at `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-25/rootless-texture-policy/`.

Listening comparisons capture raw PCM before public export normalization and retain one common playback gain. The Saints reference keeps 35/34/51/40 piano attacks in Swing/Straight/Bossa/Ballad; deliberate three-note rootless attacks occur 2/2/6/3 times, reducing total piano notes 129→127, 125→123, 191→185 and 154→151. A complete 52-bar Blues Swing reference keeps 28 piano attacks and reduces total notes 101→96, with five deliberately thinned rootless attacks. These counts describe scope, not musical preference.

The independent integration suite passed nine cases (1.18 seconds), including a pinned real solo-peak boundary where qualified dense evidence preserves the existing one-attack budget and the surviving rootless chord actually has three notes. No pattern, family or budget was forced in that regression. Initial broad local validation passed 6,596 tests plus 40 expected failures (27.36 seconds, four workers); the additional boundary regression passed separately. Typechecking passed with zero errors/warnings; the build completed in 5.96 seconds.

Ten matched raw full-mix WAVs and exact source inputs are retained at `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-25/rootless-texture/listen.html`. All five before/after pairs have finite, unclipped PCM and matched duration; maximum raw peak is 0.25156. Whole-mix RMS changes range from −0.00075 to −0.01258 dB. Chromium checked all ten files decode and the same-time audition controls work. These are rhythm-section-only recordings, not a melody or live-soloist listening verdict.

Final sidecar validation passed **6,597 tests plus 40 expected failures** across 424 files (67.88 seconds; 74 seconds including sync). Snapshot: `d4107133-2782-4f62-bef0-4ecbfb951448`. Hosted CircleCI was not run.

All **45 browser checks passed** across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (3.5 minutes), including actual three-note export/card evidence, full-tune audio-clock playback, native styles and two-take blind-review lifecycle. Independent final review found no actionable issue. The audition page also passed its separate Chromium decode/control checks.

A documentation-refresh build was initially invoked without the existing public test environment and failed on missing static environment exports. No source workaround was added; the refresh uses the same placeholder public environment as the validated preview. Pre-commit elapsed time is approximately 12 minutes including baseline preparation: reading about one minute, implementation three, and review/documentation three, overlapping validation. Focused runs took 0.317–1.22 seconds; the 3.5-minute browser matrix was the slowest individual check.

Next investigation: bounded reciprocal dialogue, starting with piano awareness of actually emitted drum commentary. Require strictly causal recent evidence, protected native pulse/patterns and existing silence/attack budgets, explicit checkpoint policy binding, and a full-tune audible comparison. A shared plan alone must not be presented as the musicians having heard one another. This is a proposed next increment, not implemented behavior.

## Reciprocal drum-to-piano space — 25 October 2026

Previous checkpoint: `6ff301e88338ed9e77ec665a8616e732c5f28628` (three-note rootless support), pushed and verified on `origin/musicality`; its final documentation build passed in 5.79 seconds and the refreshed preview returned HTTP 200.

The next increment targets P55 and ensemble Rule 2: a pianist leaving space after actual independent drum commentary. The fixed renderer generates all piano before drums, so this increment is explicitly limited to coordinated rolling playback with explicit form metadata. A complete drums-first rewrite using future evidence is not permitted. The fixed full-tune listening studio remains unchanged.

An initial proposal to reduce a two-attack piano cap to one proved inert across 768 ordinary performances: the existing ensemble planner had already restrained those opportunities. A density-weight-only variant would miss retained motifs and Ballad's native pattern. The selected policy is an occasional seeded one-bar breath after a qualifying multi-hit optional drum phrase, protecting arrivals, responses, endings, existing silence and native pulse, with a three-bar cooldown. No new drum gesture is invented to make a style qualify; Bossa timekeeping is not commentary.

Before edits, 144 fixed and 96 rolling complete-tune baselines were captured across Saints/Blues, four styles, three keys and two seed variants. The engine regression first reproduced an actual missed breath at AABA Swing seed 14, bar 51: one scheduled chord where the selected response required none. Presenter regressions separately reproduced missing causal explanations; a cached-read test found and fixed an omitted deep freeze of nested rootless-texture evidence. Existing tests and assertions remain intact. Verification and audition results follow as they complete; human listening acceptance remains pending.

The final comparison covers all 240 captured performances: all 144 fixed outputs and all 48 metadata-free rolling outputs remain identical. Bass and protected drum pulse are identical across 96 rolling scenarios. Six of 48 explicit rolling performances change piano events. Swing and Straight each have four selected breathing bars across twelve takes, three of which remove a previously actual attack; Bossa and Ballad have none in this reference set. This restrained result is documented rather than forcing native grooves to demonstrate the feature.

Local and sidecar full suites both passed **6,633 tests plus 40 expected failures** across 427 files (27.41 and 67.11 seconds respectively; sidecar including sync 80 seconds). Typecheck passed with zero errors/warnings; build passed in 5.79 seconds. The initial parallel typecheck encountered generated public-environment declarations being overwritten by a concurrent Vitest process, plus one test-only union inference error. The annotation was corrected without changing assertions, and final typecheck/build ran sequentially after tests. No application workaround was introduced.

The browser export regression also exposed that the lab flattened events and shared plans but discarded top-level development evidence. The export adapter now accumulates the six actual window decision arrays, including feedback, without regenerating the performance. Presenter details require matching previous scheduled drum hits and current piano evidence; a selected breath is not presented as proof that a chord was removed from a counterfactual. Independent review found no actionable issue.

Next planned integration: make Coordinated full-tune listening use the causal rolling renderer without requesting a microphone or adding another UI mode. Keep Original unchanged, publish only committed event/decision prefixes, preserve the current release tail and stable voice ownership, require complete actual dispatch before blind-review completion, and cover native styles and export provenance. Fixed offline generation parity remains a separate concern. This next integration is not yet implemented.

Final adaptive browser validation passed **37 tests with two existing Firefox native-audio skips** in 1.8 minutes. All three engines passed the strengthened actual-window/development export checks; Chromium and WebKit retained complete real-sample audio through one and three loops. The final synchronized sidecar also passed typechecking with zero errors/warnings (25.8 seconds; 30.6 seconds including sync). Snapshot: `8c258c5b-274a-4883-b977-a06239486dd4`. No hosted CircleCI ran.

The durable audition package is `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-25/drum-piano-feedback/listen.html`, with eight raw full-tune WAVs, event inputs, checksums and baseline/matrix reports. All audio is finite and unclipped, with raw peaks at most 0.25061. Swing and Straight each remove one previously scheduled piano attack; whole-mix RMS changes are −0.00986 and −0.01190 dB. Event-identical Bossa and Ballad controls show small renderer differences (−0.00132 and −0.00177 dB); waveform differences must not all be attributed to the new policy. Nothing is independently peak-normalized for this comparison.

Pre-commit elapsed time is about thirteen minutes, with approximate reading/policy investigation four minutes, implementation two, and review/documentation three, overlapping validation and raw rendering. Focused engine/presenter/integration runs took about 0.3–2 seconds; local full tests 27.41 seconds, remote full tests 67.11 seconds and browser checks 1.8 minutes. The full-tune raw rendering and browser work overlap; these stages are not additive. Human listening acceptance remains pending. The following checkpoint records this increment's verified commit SHA.

## Causal full-tune listening — 25 October 2026

Previous checkpoint: `9c083c52fde68771c984bf1e5fc597064c7dbc84` (reciprocal drum-to-piano space), pushed and verified on `origin/musicality`; final documentation build passed in 5.78 seconds and the refreshed preview returned HTTP 200.

Intent: the existing Coordinated choice in the full-tune studio and blind comparison will use the causal rolling rhythm section, so the previous milestone becomes available over real tunes with audio-clock musician decisions. Original remains on its fixed renderer. No microphone request or extra arrangement mode is introduced. The fixed offline generator is a separate path, not silently made reciprocal.

The runtime must retain actual committed event/decision prefixes, stable take ownership, bounded scheduler work, truthful errors, and the existing 2.5-second ending tail. Completion requires every bar and dispatch, not elapsed time alone. The UI follows sounding bars while scheduling can look ahead; early exports must identify their committed extent. The two curated 4/4 tunes fit all existing one-to-four solo choices: Saints 48/64/80/96 bars and Blues 40/52/64/76 bars, below the unchanged 128-bar source limit.

Initial browser red reproduced missing causal playback provenance on the old studio. Audio regressions cover ending-tail retention, incomplete final dispatch, and ownership after cancellation or replacement. Implementation and focused evidence continue below; human musical acceptance remains pending.

Implementation now routes the existing Coordinated choice and blind-comparison player through the rolling renderer, with an independent 25 ms scheduling pump and audio-clock display refresh. No microphone observer or new mode is added. Actual committed windows are projected into immutable musician evidence; partial exports identify their extent. A completed take requires the complete form, actual successful dispatch and the existing 2.5-second release tail. Physical output ownership is separate from cancellation validity, preserving cleanup without stopping a newer take.

Behavioral reds covered missing causal provenance, release tails, incomplete dispatch, cancellation ownership and blind archive acceptance. Focused audio tests passed 55 cases across five files; player/comparison tests passed 39 cases (1.86 seconds), and the independent complete-form integration passed 33 cases (5.57 seconds). The latter composes actual generation, session and renderer across every current tune/chorus choice and all four styles. Existing archive size bounds and historical exports remain supported.

Full local validation (`npm test -- --maxWorkers=4`) passed **6,704 tests plus 40 expected failures** across 430 files in 28.52 seconds. `chunk validate test` passed the same counts on the sidecar in 76.68 seconds (91 seconds including synchronization). Local placeholder-environment `npm run check` reported zero errors/warnings; `npm run build` passed in 5.74 seconds. The synchronized sidecar check also reported zero errors/warnings (37.5 seconds; 41.6 seconds including sync). No hosted CircleCI ran.

An independent read-only source review found no actionable issue in cancellation ownership, complete-dispatch/tail gates, immutable prefix projection, archive validation or Original scheduling. No pull request or hosted review was started. Browser validation and retained full-tune evidence follow below.

Focused commands were `npx vitest run tests/unit/audio/backing-rolling-session.test.ts tests/unit/audio/backing-rolling-renderer.test.ts tests/unit/audio/backing-rolling-take.test.ts tests/unit/audio/backing-track-coverage.test.ts tests/unit/audio/backing-track-supersede.test.ts tests/unit/audio/backing-track-failure-lifecycle.test.ts`, `npx vitest run tests/unit/musicality/listening-player.test.ts tests/unit/musicality/listening-player-browser.test.ts tests/unit/musicality/listening-comparison.test.ts`, and `npx vitest run tests/unit/musicality/listening-causal-integration.test.ts`. The extra audio `backing-track-failure-lifecycle` filter matched no file; the reported five-file coverage excludes it. Focused commands used the same placeholder public environment, without `PLAYWRIGHT=1`. Local check/build and sidecar check use `PLAYWRIGHT=1 PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=https://example.supabase.co PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY=example-public-test-key`; the sidecar command is `chunk validate --remote --cmd '<environment> npm run check'`.

The full browser matrix passed **42/42 checks** across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit in **6.3 minutes**, with `env PLAYWRIGHT_PORT=4387 PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=https://example.supabase.co PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY=example-public-test-key npx playwright test tests/e2e/musicality-listening.spec.ts tests/e2e/musicality-comparison.spec.ts --workers=3`. Each engine completed the four styles, blind pairs, actual drum-to-piano cue, partial/final export checks, cancellation/navigation and deadline recovery. ## Bounded rolling bass approach entry — 25 October 2026

Previous checkpoint: `962707108154c5980b93601db41d6dbcd7bf52dc` (one-use adjacent piano phrase recall), pushed and verified on `origin/musicality`.

Intent: remove a concrete avoidable register lurch before considering broader B30 contour. The 18-semitone fall into the selected chromatic-above approach was not an accepted launch or ornament. Fixed generation already connected the same anchors more smoothly; that route was evidence of feasibility, not a route to hardcode. All 240 baseline performances and eight native full-mix/bass-stem graphs were captured before implementation.

The ordinary-first wrapper admits only complete four-beat aligned walking segments with two free interior quarters before a selected one-note chromatic approach. Already-safe ordinary paths remain exact. A pure alternate from the same checkpoint filters the existing ordered candidate pools for a reachable strong-beat chord tone, then uses the unchanged seeded weights. Each hop from the original downbeat through the approach must remain within five semitones. Downbeat, approach and next target remain fixed. Empty pools, missing or rejected pulses, or any event count/timing/duration/velocity difference retain the entire ordinary result and its history. Existing ghost echoes can follow their parent pitch without moving. Accepted/planned launches, reserved setup/arrival roles, another response owner, two-feel and two-note approaches stay protected. ## Explicit terminal hold and release — 25 October 2026

Previous checkpoint: `0c4ed4c08ad1495eeddd5c90e506de81e4c909ed` (bounded bass approach entry), pushed and verified on `origin/musicality`.

Intent: add a declared closing gesture to a complete arrangement, rather than infer a stop from an ending energy label. The prior read-only audit captured the natural 52-bar Blues C ending: final C6, tied melody C4 through the middle of the last bar, C2→G1 bass, no piano attack and native drum time continuing. These are valid existing events. The missing capability is a shared terminal arrangement instruction under P15/D65 and ensemble Rule 4, not a pitch correction or general stop-time implementation. Continue time remains the default and all earlier repeat seams continue normally.

The selected scope is explicit Coordinated Swing/Straight in 4/4, fixed and rolling, with an opt-in Hold & release choice in the listening studio. The engine requires known melody information, a complete single supported final-bar harmony, and no new untied melody attack after the final downbeat. A tied sustain can determine a hold of two to four beats. The gesture uses a common nominal final downbeat, root bass, the current piano harmony/family and restrained ride/kick punctuation. A pure candidate may replace the ordinary final bar only as a complete group; unresolved candidates or preceding fixed notes that overlap its cue retain the whole ordinary result. The accepted plan explicitly suspends ordinary pulse/time for this arranged space. The actual emitted events alone update memory and support accepted explanations. No prior event is clipped, no scheduler completion rule changes, and the existing release tail remains.

Baseline and native captures precede implementation. Omitted/Continue intent, Original, metadata-free inputs, Bossa and Ballad must remain exact controls; unsupported ending selections explain their unavailability. The shared terminal identity belongs only to the absolute final declared loop and binds opted-in checkpoints. Final implementation, regressions, validation and audition results follow as the milestone completes; human listening acceptance remains pending.


Contract refinement from independent review: a final one-bar section can already have an incoming shared arrival or assigned response. Overwriting only its events would leave contradictory ownership, while rewriting prior reservations would violate causal history. The complete terminal cue therefore withholds on existing setup/arrival, committed launch or non-empty response ownership. Ordinary terminal silence/density may be replaced only in an unowned bar. This conservative admission preserves existing counters and committed transitions; the natural Blues witness remains admitted. No old requirement or regression assertion was weakened.

## MagicPath listening desk and Musicality 0.1.0 — 29 October 2026

Baseline: `545c67b1a1e69c0c997d6232fdf897a8cd066978`, the verified native Ballad reply on `musicality`. Intent: make full-tune evaluation easier without changing musical behavior, and begin versioning musicality releases independently of app builds. User requested the current MagicPath redesign, authorized designer coordination, then asked for release versioning from the current baseline onward.

The designer chat “Add /musicality to the canvas” supplied MagicPath project `459408445339230208`, component `466643117894959104`, pinned revision `466647037140672512`, plus a local implementation handoff. At our request the designer added compact release identification, pin/follow, marked moments, verdict/save/export and blind evaluation controls. The Svelte implementation uses the existing actual audio engine, real lead-sheet renderer, source chart mapping and factual musician presenter. It does not copy the prototype's simulated player. Score and musicians share the desktop view; mobile keeps notation readable in a bounded, keyboard-scrollable score well. Full settings, terminal arrangement, local tune book, same-document specification links and the detailed ledger remain available. Existing status counts were not promoted by this UI work.

`release.ts` defines Musicality **0.1.0**, a listening preview of engine baseline `545c67b1`. New takes capture this immutable identity; complete blind comparisons require matching captured identities, and reveal/export/archive retain it. Old evidence remains explicitly unversioned. Build identity and schema versions are separate. Subsequent musical behavior additions advance the minor version; compatible musical corrections advance the patch; an incompatible musical contract advances the major. UI-only changes retain the musical release. No historical release was invented and no musical acceptance is claimed.

Ordinary listening feedback retains the most recent 20 summaries per browser account namespace, with captured release/build, tune, style, key, tempo, solo count, phrase ID, swing, volume, mix, ending and instrument. The summary records only reached bars, complete/partial status, up to 50 timestamped marks, notes and a listener verdict. It is not an audio replay or a reproducible chart archive; full take export retains the existing actual parameters, generated score, causal provenance, decisions and optional review cursor. Mark moment captures the current sounding bar immediately and opens an editable note. Pinning/review changes presentation only and never seeks, restarts or relabels audio. Account changes invalidate the active save boundary. Browser storage failures are surfaced rather than reported as saves. Existing blind archives keep their prior browser-local storage contract.

Independent implementation/review covered release provenance, chart mapping, feedback validation, account boundaries, and mobile readability. Behavioral TDD: release/comparison tests first produced five intended identity failures, then 44/44 passed; score mapping first produced four semantic failures, then the 30-case score/tune partition passed; feedback tests covered unsounded/future/mismatched takes, final-bar boundary, Svelte proxy copies, account namespaces and frozen capture. Final captured-settings tests produced two intended failures before 19/19 passed. The first browser desk test failed at the absent release badge; the first quick-mark test failed at the absent editable marked note. Both were implemented and rerun.

The first selected cross-browser regression matrix passed 24/33. Nine failures were the same three presentation issues in each engine: the planned-arc qualifier disappeared, review evidence gained extra sentence text inside an exact label, and the old chord-grid selector no longer targeted the replacement real score. Restored the two semantic labels; migrated only the chord locator to the actual notation while retaining exact transposed DΔ7/GΔ7 assertions. Existing musical hashes, decision details, complete-tune assertions and causal scheduling checks were not weakened. Mobile review found unreadable shrunken notation; a 650px minimum sheet inside a bounded scroll region fixes readability, with keyboard scrolling and page-overflow assertions.

Validation artifacts: `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-29/redesign/`. Broad local `npm test -- --maxWorkers=4` passed **7,424 tests plus 40 existing expected failures / 464 files**, **32.25 seconds**. Bundle-based `chunk validate --remote --cmd 'npm test -- --maxWorkers=4 && PLAYWRIGHT=1 PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=https://example.supabase.co PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY=example-public-test-key npm run check'` passed the same broad suite in **82.56 seconds**, then zero type errors/warnings; **2m1s command / 2m51s including bundle sync**. No hosted CircleCI was triggered. Final affected checks and browser reruns are recorded below after completion.

Audition guidance: start with one full curated tune at a familiar tempo, then use an eligible saved tune-book chart. Compare Original and Coordinated with the same key, tempo, solo count and mix; use blind A/B when judging preference. Listen through head-in, solo development and head-out. Mark moments that support or interrupt the phrase, then add a short note and verdict after stopping. Pin a musician card to examine its actual scheduled evidence without interrupting playback. Repeat across Swing, Straight, Bossa and Ballad, paying attention to each style's pulse and space. Compare version-labelled feedback over time, while treating changed settings or partial listens as distinct evidence. Human listening acceptance remains pending; this release adds evaluation tools, not proof of musical excellence.

## 2026-10-29 — Whole-tune arc, lighter evidence polling and callback diagnostics

Intent: make the overall performance trajectory visible above the rhythm section, rename the ending control to “Keep groove to the end”, and improve long-performance runtime reliability without changing already auditioned music. The user reports liking what they hear; this is encouraging qualitative feedback, not acceptance of every style or roadmap item. Work remains on musicality, using local free test execution and the sidecar; no hosted CircleCI or dev/main/production writes.

The right panel now contains a compact full-form energy line with proportional Head in / Solo / Head out spans and a current-bar marker. It derives from the actual declared performance and the existing engine energy policy. Pinning and silent review move the same marker without seeking audio. Original retains form and location but no invented orchestration curve. The label explicitly distinguishes structural intention from loudness, and independent review prompted a textual start/peak/end description for screen readers. The ending option changes only its label; saved `continue` values keep their exact semantics.

Independent renderer implementation removes repeated snapshot/history copying when clock polling has not changed musical evidence. Unchanged dispatch rows are shared safely; changed rows and old snapshots remain frozen. All transition paths invalidate the cache. Failed or late dispatches now retain optional finite, JSON-safe reason and observed/target/callback time fields. The exact scheduling guards, compute budgets, event generation and checkpoint bindings are unchanged. Commit-time history sorting remains separate future work. Musicality stays **0.1.0** because the performed musical output is unchanged; application build identity distinguishes this delivery.

Behavioral validation: renderer caching/diagnostics produced nine semantic failures before implementation, then **23/23** passed. Independent full-performance contracts cover all four styles at 52/128 bars (720 bars total), exact replay through JSON-restored checkpoints, generated export equality, immutable historical rows and no scheduling/generation during repeated polling: **10/10**, 1.809s test / 2.67s runner / 3.729s wrapper. Arc projection had seven semantic failures and one compatibility pass before implementation, then **16/16** including existing energy tests, 176ms runner / 1.21s wrapper. No historical regression assertion was weakened.

A non-gating benchmark of the actual transpiled renderer, with 128 bars, 1,024 played dispatches and 10,000 unchanged snapshot reads, measured **334.599416 ms before / 0.441167 ms after** on Node 24.3 Darwin. Serialized before/after output SHA-256 is identical: `bbcc90f807cd39f8e28e62f9220ed2c949acc59a9e865fb300b4996e385d05d6`. This proves removal of redundant stable-read work in that benchmark, not the cause or resolution of previous browser timing flakes.

Broad local validation: `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=4 circleci testsuite run 'unit tests' --local --run-tests=all --analyze-tests=none` passed **8,089 tests plus 40 existing expected failures / 507 files**, **38.41s runner / 40.464s wrapper**. Local typecheck reported zero errors and warnings. Initial sidecar check/build passed, **1m16s command / 1m56s including sync**. Evidence is retained in `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-29/performance-arc/`.

The first browser snapshot exposed one feedback-persistence flake: after reload, “Saved feedback 1 +” remained closed despite the click; the unchanged retry passed and the data was retained. A deterministic startup-module hold tests disabled SSR state then successful opening after hydration. The existing persistence/export assertions remain intact. Any recurring timing stop now has more precise diagnostic evidence; earlier WebKit late callbacks remain an unresolved historical concern until reproduced and diagnosed.

## 2026-10-29 — Audible returning-head piano breath (0.2.0 candidate)

Intent: give the returning melody a clearer change of texture over a complete tune. Preserve the arrival, admit piano space in the next two bars, then return to ordinary native comping when allowed. This is deliberately a musical behavior increment after the 0.1.0 arc/monitoring work, not another visualization-only change. No human acceptance is inferred.

Scope: the Coordinated `/musicality` player (including curated blind A/B) explicitly opts into `RollingBackingOptions.returningMelodySpace`. Eligible heads immediately follow a solo, last at least eight bars, and begin with a section of at least four bars. Cue geometry is known before generation; no future played notes or solo observations are consulted. Planner budgets, rather than post-generation note removal, implement the space. Reserved responses, arrivals, setups/fills, terminal gestures and already stronger silence retain priority. Bass and drum time remain; later piano voicing/figure history and responsive drum commentary may change causally. Fixed/Original, default/false rolling callers, ordinary practice and the advanced lab retain their previous policy. Each style resumes its own native pattern and Bossa clave phase. This is not a promise of exactly two acoustically silent bars or a guaranteed chord at offset three: existing silence can extend the gap and reservations can withhold the new gesture.

Before implementation, captured complete baseline events from commit `0dafaca29ef4a8992fdfa289f4827ea6770d235f` for Mankunku Blues (52 bars) and a saved AABA chart (128 bars) in all four styles. Natural tempos were Blues 140, Ballad Blues 80, AABA 100 BPM. All eight witnesses contain real piano attacks removed by the new gap and actual native comping afterward. At 240 BPM the browser witnesses also contain admitted budgets at both gap bars and actual comping at offset three. Matched pre-change event JSON and provenance are retained under `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-10-29/head-return-space/`; these are evidence, not recorded human auditions.

## 2026-10-29 — Save listening notes during playback

User feedback: the energy change was difficult to hear and repeating a rise/fall every chorus risks sounding formulaic. Source inspection confirms the nominal energy planner repeats a chorus-local contour. Longer, varied waves crossing chorus boundaries remain a future musical milestone; this repair does not change musical output, the arc or release 0.2.0.

Reported blocker: Save feedback was disabled or failed. The definite reproduced cause is the UI disabling Save for the entire playing/loading state, with no adjacent explanation. The stopped-only feedback API also rejected a live capture. No specific storage failure in the user's browser has been established.

Change: the studio explicitly opts into a live partial feedback snapshot. It copies only reached, clock/provenance-validated evidence, retains captured settings/release and account boundaries, and does not stop playback or enable silent decision review during playback. The default stopped-take API and historical tests remain unchanged. Count-in/pre-play disabled states explain their prerequisite. Scope changes, invalid evidence and storage failures now have separate recovery messages; a failed save clears stale success, preserves the editable notes, and leaves Export take available. Existing browser-only storage/export semantics are stated in the notes panel. Nothing is submitted remotely.

Behavioral regression: the new live-capture contract failed with the old implementation (`Stop a played take before saving feedback`); it passes after the opt-in. Added tests cover nonmutation, partial completion even at the last bar, unsupported states, absent provenance, future marks and mismatched tunes. Browser coverage exercises actual playback continuing after save, persisted partial notes, injected storage write failure, export recovery and a successful stopped retry. The injected quota failure verifies recovery, not the cause of the user's reported failure.

Audition/use: reload /musicality, open Listening notes & take export, play a tune, enter a note and save while the bar cursor continues. Inspect Saved feedback, then reload and confirm the note remains. Exports are still required to share feedback. Musical acceptance remains pending; this increment is a usability repair, not evidence of improved musicality.

## 2026-11-08 — Longer solo waves and right-panel listening mixer (0.3.0 candidate)

Intent: build on the user's positive 0.2.0 listening response, make development less tied to repeated chorus arches, and let the listener isolate bass, piano and drums during full-tune auditions. Musicality branch only; no hosted CircleCI or production delivery.

Engine: opt-in uint32 `performanceEnergySeed` drives a separate arrangement stream across contiguous solo choruses. Varied opening delays, builds, crests, releases and low plateaus depend on elapsed music and first-solo length rather than future chorus count. The studio and blind pair capture the seed; replay and the plotted arc share it, while New energy arc chooses another. Heads retain their previous targets and the final closing phrase retains release. Native styles and energy ceilings remain. Changing solo history can change later head events; no blanket head-event equivalence is claimed. Omitted seeds preserve historical behavior and hashes, and fingerprints reject incompatible checkpoints.

### 2026-11-08 — Steady accompaniment as the 0.3.0 midpoint (delivered)

Andy explicitly approved replacing the sparse default with more steady accompaniment typical of rhythm sections, retaining occasional extreme sparseness. The preceding piano-presence refinement (`bf70ec57559114787c02a1b33f47b4dab3c6be0f`, engine `fb911b73d916d32c37c20b76577d261701c3ec93`) remains too sparse as a midpoint: seed 42 Swing Blues at 140 BPM has 30 attacks across 52 bars, 24 attack-free bars, and a longest empty run of 4. Tests established internal contracts, not an appropriate normal density.

Research basis: Jeb Patton's published Horace Silver transcription of Blowin' the Blues Away contains 2–4 right-hand chord attacks in each bar of the first 12-bar chorus, plus left-hand punctuation; the Charleston supplies a standard two-hit swing cell. These selected examples justify a fuller reference, not a universal statistical normal range. Sergio Pamies's Wynton Kelly study also describes simpler short chords under busy solos, longer values in other contexts, and coordinated piano/drum figures. Sources: https://www.shermusic.com/products/an-approach-to-comping-the-essentials ; https://jazz-hitz.musikene.eus/index.php/jazz-hitz/article/download/85/49/ ; https://www.pianogroove.com/jazz-piano-lessons/comping-voicings-rhythms/ . The original spec is retained verbatim; the user's clarification supersedes interpreting its silence guidance as frequent obligatory multi-bar withdrawal.

Implementation intent: a new opt-in steady policy keeps previous `pianoPresence: true` and omitted-option behavior reproducible. Ordinary light support should admit familiar two-attack native cells, use thinner/quieter voicings for restraint, and avoid compounded accidental rests. A minority of seeded phrases may deliberately become very sparse. Exact reservations, actual solo observations, style vocabulary, causal scheduling and checkpoint replay remain binding. Root owns listening integration/ledger/presenter; independent agents implement and review musical contracts. All checks run locally, with isolated local Colima/Docker browsers; no sidecars or hosted pipelines.

Audition: complete two or more solo choruses, judging the ordinary accompaniment between contrasts. Piano should be reliably present in the groove, with natural varying figures rather than a compulsory chord each bar. Sparse passages should sound like a deliberate change of texture, followed by supported playing. Compare Swing/Straight rhythmic continuity, Bossa native cells and Ballad sustained support. Human acceptance remains pending.


Engine implementation: the new `pianoPresence: 'steady'` policy admits two attacks in ordinary bars before protected interaction gates. Reduced discretionary rest weighting and connected motif reuse let existing native figures carry a phrase; a retained figure that no longer fits may be replaced by a complete fitting cell before realization. No chords are appended after arbitration. A minority of seeded four-bar solo phrases deliberately thin to quiet tails, excluding adjacent contrasts and the tail immediately before a returning head. The previous `true` option remains byte-identical; policy and seed bind checkpoint identity. The live musician panel labels texture as planned, separately from actual played support.

Runtime forwarding and the two planned-texture presenter contracts failed before integration, then passed 334/334 across 2 files (1.11s runner /2.161s wrapper). No existing regression assertion was relaxed.

### 2026-11-09 — Voicing/texture contrast and measured organ balance (delivered)

Intent: Andy requests the next piano/organ improvements to focus on voicing and texture variety, and to level organ volume against piano. The 0.5.0 work varied rhythm and articulation while retaining the prior chord language. Current family selection is seeded by harmony segment, and the resolver strongly favors minimal guide-tone movement/common tones; changing register targets alone would not reliably produce audible variety. The two sample banks also currently share the same comp trim despite different measured levels.

Plan: introduce a separate, fingerprinted keyboardTexture opt-in for deliberate light/compact/open rootless palette contrasts within eligible Swing/Straight accompaniment. Choose actual pitch sets and spacing before voice-leading, preserving the defining tones, bar timing and ordinary support; dense evidence, background response harmony, arrivals, replies and terminal reservations retain priority. Keep historical policies and native Bossa/Ballad generation unchanged. Independently measure identical legal voicings/rhythms through real Piano and Organ sample banks over short/held passages and registers, then use one measured gain policy in live and offline bounce paths. User faders/mute retain their meaning. Do not normalize each chord independently or equate peak equality with perceived balance.

Independent agents own musical engine contracts, organ measurements/gain lifecycle and read-only review; root owns release, listening integration and factual musician view. All commands this increment use direct local npm/Vitest and the repository local Docker browser runner: no CircleCI CLI, hosted jobs or sidecars. Initial runtime contract fails because keyboardTexture is not yet forwarded. Release will advance to 0.6.0 after an engine checkpoint; original notes/feedback identities remain captured, never relabelled.

Audition: a complete Coordinated Swing Mankunku Blues in F and Rhythm Changes, then Straight. Compare thinner three-note support with compact/fuller chords and wider shapes across phrases, including answering cells, while checking that harmonic support stays regular. Switch Piano/Organ at the same 100% keyboard fader and overall volume; compare both short comping and held chords in the full mix. Native Bossa/Ballad keep their musical character and receive only the instrument-level balance correction. Human acceptance of texture and perceived balance remains pending.


Engine implementation: a seeded phrase palette now admits rootless A/B on plain 7/maj7/min7 harmony even when the previous segment draw selected shell/drop-2/quartal. An initial same-harmony behavioral test exposed that restricting the policy to already-rootless choices could leave an entire sustained segment unchanged. A twenty-percent seeded phrase choice retains the original family color; remaining phrases contrast light three-note, compact and open shapes, with complementary answer profiles. Existing three-note permissions still cap fuller profiles. Candidate spacing is compact ≤12 or open ≥14 semitones within the existing 19-semitone / MIDI 48–84 limits. An excess over 4 semitones of guide-motion cost declines the spacing request. Actual decision trace records applied spacing/fallback and the visible card derives its note count/span from matching emitted notes. No extra piano attacks or changes to bass/drum events.

Focused texture contracts: 45/45 across 4 files, 4.77 s. Default combined-policy Swing Blues / 140 BPM / seed 42 has 37 three-note and 11 four-note policy chords, 6 open and 36 compact placements, 6 voice-leading fallbacks and 64 changed chord-note arrays versus 0.5. Across four seeds, each Swing/Straight Blues has 131 thin / 44 fuller, 47 open / 112 compact; AABA 433 thin / 121 fuller, 108 open / 362 compact. Tests pin actual held-harmony variation, historical all-style hashes, support/timing/bass/drum identity, replay, loops, dense/future evidence and policy isolation. Independent final review found no blocker.

Organ calibration: matched native sample phrases initially showed substantial level differences. Short/held test cases alone suggested a larger gain; adding the requested long 2.4–2.7 s pads exposed the natural envelope difference. Final shared output multiplier is 2.18 (+6.77 dB), piano trim 0.1 unchanged, organ 0.218. Six matched two-/three-/four-note/register/duration/velocity cases measure organ-minus-piano RMS from −1.94 to +1.89 dB across Chromium/Firefox/WebKit. Maximum organ peak 0.04084, piano 0.06919. Live and bounce use the same helper; accepted instrument replacement updates calibration only after successful/current loading. Faders, mute, failed/stale replacement and switching back are tested; generated velocity/duration data is unchanged. Correction applies to Original/Coordinated and every style, so old organ audio level intentionally changes even though historical events remain reproducible. No per-chord normalization or equal-perceived-loudness claim.

### 2026-11-09 — Connected keyboard phrases and native instrument articulation (delivered)

Intent: Andy likes 0.6.0 and authorizes 0.7.0: more connected keyboard voice leading, distinct Piano/Organ articulation, and extension to Bossa/Ballad using their own rhythmic character. Preserve regular harmonic support, native rhythm ownership, causal scheduling/replay and Original compatibility. Positive feedback on 0.6.0 is acknowledged; 0.7.0 listening acceptance remains pending.

Implementation: captured `keyboardPhrasing: 'piano' | 'organ'` participates in the rolling fingerprint. Seeded short contours use actual previous notes and native phrase pacing; candidate selection admits only a strict improvement in top-note target distance within guide-motion +2 / total movement +4 and style-specific top-leap bounds. Native textures favor compact/light Bossa and retain most original Ballad families. Distinct articulation changes durations only, with bounded sustain and actual-onset/harmony/bar clipping. No extra attacks, velocities, bass or drum changes are intended. Existing response/background/arrival/dense constraints and terminal release ownership retain priority. Exact emitted notes and gate durations support factual musician-card explanations.

Independent work: one agent owns the pure line policy/resolver and contracts, another articulation and integrated replay/protection contracts, and another reviews integration and adds real-sample full-tune balance tests. Root owns rolling/listening integration, native textures, full-form behavioral witnesses, ledger/docs/release and local validation. All validation uses direct local npm/Vitest and the isolated Docker browser runner; no CircleCI CLI, hosted jobs or sidecars.

Behavioral TDD: all-four-style integration and runtime forwarding initially failed because the new option was ignored. Current 0.6.0 full-output hashes were captured before edits and remain exact. Presenter tests first failed for missing new descriptions, then passed after actual-note/duration matching. An initial typecheck caught widened `family: string` in native texture input; the input now uses the helper's explicit parameter type. No musical assertion was relaxed. Independent integrated review found no blocker.

Audition: full Blues and Rhythm Changes, first Swing/Straight then Bossa/Ballad, Piano and Organ at the same faders. Listen for connected upper notes across chord changes without lost support, compact repeated Bossa colors over its unchanged syncopation, and slower Ballad motion with sustained harmony. Compare the organ's longer releases against the piano's shorter articulation in the whole mix; isolated RMS is not perceived-volume acceptance.


Complete-form witnesses across seeds 42, 7 and 19: Swing and Straight each produce 12 strictly improved top-note choices / 79 changed chord arrays on Blues, and 15 / 143 on AABA; Bossa 20 / 169 and 14 / 411; Ballad 22 / 156 and 20 / 332. Changed later chords include causal consequences of actual voiced history and native texture changes; they are not all direct contour interventions. Every connected selection preserves its admitted chord tones/spacing and satisfies the explicit detour bounds. Native onsets, attack counts, velocities, bass and drum events are exact against 0.6.0 in the full default takes. Piano/Organ pitches are identical; their gates differ. Native texture witnesses are present in both Bossa and Ballad.

Local focused results: line/resolver/texture 30/30 in 4.22 s; articulation/level 28/28 in 382 ms; integrated full-form witnesses 13/13 in 4.71 s; presenter/routing 352/352 in 1.14 s; replay/protection plus articulation 74/74 in 7.37 s. Integrated protection contracts cover actual arrivals/background and exact terminal hold gates. Sampled full tunes did not emit an accepted piano reply, so the additional Ballad candidate contract uses an actually generated ordinary pad, native timing and history; existing source-admission regressions remain intact. This is not new listening evidence for reply frequency.

Broad local `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=4 npm test -- --run` passed 8,392 plus 40 existing expected failures across 524 files in 49.41 s. `PLAYWRIGHT=1 PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=https://example.supabase.co PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY=example-public-test-key npm run check` passed zero errors and warnings after the type correction. Logs are retained under `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-11-09/connected-keyboard/`.


Native-sample validation found a real balance regression before delivery: Ballad organ measured about +2.24 dB against piano over the full new take, beyond the unchanged ±2 dB contract. Swing, Straight and Bossa passed. The organ whole-pad extension was inappropriate for its sustained envelope versus the piano decay. Revised Ballad-only articulation releases whole pads (at least three written beats) at 95% of the written gate, leaving a small breath; shorter pads/pickups use 104%. Thus 3.6 beats becomes 3.42, while 1.8 becomes 1.872. Protected support/replies/arrivals and global organ gain 2.18 remain unchanged. New behavioral assertions failed under the original extension before this correction. The same full-sample balance and tail assertions will be rerun; no tolerance is relaxed. The fixture parity test independently passed 1/1, matching all 52 bars, harmony, section provenance, performance form and phrase ID to the live listening session.


The first Ballad correction (95% whole-pad gate) measured +2.0004 to +2.0006 dB in Chromium/Firefox and approximately +1.98 dB in WebKit: still on the unchanged boundary, so it was not accepted. The final proposed whole-pad gate is 90% (3.6→3.24 beats), retaining over three beats of harmony and a clear closing breath; shorter pads/pickups remain 104%. The additional focused articulation/level batch passed 28/28 after a red/green behavioral check. The three unaffected styles passed all three native engines in both sample runs; final Ballad-only remeasurement follows. Both unsuccessful measurements and the original harness failure remain in the artifact folder. This is envelope refinement, not a per-note loudness normalization or a change to the established global gain.

### 2026-11-10 — Ensemble development, listening release 0.8.0 (listening preview)

Intent: Andy reports that 0.7.0’s connected phrases sound great and requests one improvement from the plan for each rhythm-section member. Positive listening feedback on connected phrases is recorded; it does not establish acceptance of the new release or all styles. Selected scope: B35 delayed-root arrivals on repeated harmony, P33 contrary inner-voice movement beneath the connected keyboard top, and D85 drum cushioning at selected structural resolutions. Swing, Straight, Bossa and Ballad retain their native rhythmic character. No CircleCI CLI, sidecars, hosted runs, dev/main changes or deployment.

Implementation is independently split by instrument, with shared rolling policy identity, integration, factual musician cards and full-performance validation owned by the root agent. Each opt-in has a separate fingerprint field; omission must retain exact 0.7.0 output. Initial integration contracts failed for all four ignored policies and live-player forwarding, establishing red baselines. Full 0.7.0 output hashes were captured before the changes.

Audition target: full Mankunku Blues in F and Rhythm Changes, Piano and Organ, then Bossa and Ballad. Listen for occasional chord-tone bass openings resolving to the root without lost pulse, inner chord movement against the keyboard top, and a drummer easing around a held resolution without losing the groove. These should be selected gestures rather than a mandatory event every phrase. Human acceptance of 0.8.0 remains pending.


Full-tune red/green refinement: initial per-tune expectations exposed admission that was too restrictive, and an incorrect assumption that every device must appear in every tune. The actual Blues has little repeated same-quality harmony, so bass admission now also develops an existing third/fifth target without moving that approach-owned entrance. Bossa’s seed42 Blues still declines its two safe common-fifth opportunities; its native AABA has actual delayed roots. No probability was raised to force those two bars. Final opportunity draws are 65% on repeated harmony / 50% on safe changes, behind all ownership and pitch guards. Newly moved entrances stay within five semitones; preserved ordinary entrances may retain their existing seven-semitone step; the root landing allows a diminished-fifth distance of six, and adjacent emitted notes remain within seven.

Contrary-motion opportunities are naturally rare. A redundant random/phrase-offset gate suppressed the legal Ballad example, so first-ordinary-attack admission relies on harmonic, register, line and movement constraints rather than another probability draw. The exact chosen top is preferred, with an equally distant target fallback where legal. Across full Blues plus AABA, seeds 42/7/19, changes are Swing 3 / Straight 3 / Bossa 6 / Ballad 1; already-existing contrary pairs are retained. Tests assert native style reachability across full forms rather than manufacturing a change in each take. No prior regression assertion was weakened.

Drum admission initially missed actual phrase-resolution restraint and native half-bar pads. It now permits the existing phrase_resolution restraint while keeping dense/other reservations, and eases inside the accepted keyboard gate’s nominal beat window. Actual shared timing still owns ticks. Swing/Straight soften ride by 14%, Bossa offbeat hats by 22%, Ballad ride by 18%; optional complete commentary cells/ride skips may be withheld where applicable, while native pulse/clave/kick/pedal remain. Current keyboard receipt stores exact scheduled time and duration for factual presentation.

Independent review found no engine blocker. Two defensive fixes were applied: terminal trace replacement drops any prior cushioning receipt, and the contrary-voice card rejects nonfinite motion costs. New card tests reject mismatched prior/actual notes, missing root landings, wrong source gates and changed drum-hit evidence. Export aggregation retains detached, immutable delayed-root receipts.

Focused validation: keyboard 29/29 in 6.23 s; bass 21/21 in 1.55 s; drum 43/43 in 846 ms; presenter/routing/export 55/55 in 1.11 s. Combined full-form, loop, future-evidence, dense and terminal protection tests passed before broad validation. Initial broad run used the still-in-progress Bossa per-tune test and failed only those two assertions; the finalized test uses native AABA rather than forcing the device into Blues. An overlapping local Vitest/typecheck run rewrote generated Svelte environment declarations, causing five missing placeholder-env export errors; typecheck is rerun sequentially after tests. No application code or environment secret was changed for that tooling race.

Final broad local unit validation: `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=4 npm test -- --run` passed 8,475 tests plus 40 existing expected failures across 532 files in 54.02 s. Sequential `PLAYWRIGHT=1 PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL=https://example.supabase.co PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY=example-public-test-key npm run check` passed zero errors and warnings. The full 0.7.0 output hashes remain exact with all three new options omitted. Combined seeds 42/7/19 across Blues and AABA produce delayed roots / changed contrary chords / cushioned bars: Swing 49/3/6, Straight 49/3/6, Bossa 96/6/14, Ballad 90/1/6. Every bar replayed from JSON; the finite loop seam, current dense evidence, future-evidence isolation and both instruments’ terminal holds passed.

Seed42 full Blues audition map (one-based performed bars, including the four-bar intro): Swing/Straight delayed roots at 3, 10, 13, 46; Ballad at 6, 11. The selected drum easing occurs at 20 in each style. This seed retains existing contrary motion rather than adding a new inner choice; seed19 demonstrates new contrary movement in Swing/Straight, Bossa and Ballad.
