### 2026-11-10 — WebKit playback reliability before 0.9.0 — 0.8.1

Intent: fix the intermittent WebKit playback stops before any new musical release. Work remains on musicality, with direct local npm checks and the isolated local Colima/Docker browser runner only. No CircleCI CLI, hosted pipelines or sidecars. Existing 0.8 failures and the unchanged 0.7 one-worker failure remain recorded above; no musical acceptance is inferred from browser tests.

Diagnosis: temporary stage instrumentation (saved outside the repository and removed before delivery) measures checkpoint validation/copy, ensemble planning, timing/feedback, piano, bass, drums, recovery/terminal, finalization and renderer commit. The first instrumented WebKit Piano full 52-bar take completed, but generation reached the unchanged 25 ms limit at bar 29 (zero-based), with bar 11 taking 19 ms. Whole-form ensemble work reached 4 ms, piano 8 ms, bass 11 ms, finalization 7 ms; these are stage maxima across different bars, not additive measurements from a single failure. This directly demonstrates exhausted headroom in a successful take, not proof that algorithm work alone explains every VM/browser wall-clock pause or the historical deadline failure. Temporary measurements and both original/prior-release failed exports are retained for comparison.

Changes under validation: detach/validate/fingerprint static ensemble bars and section indexes once before playback; privately retain immutable bass register centers, harmonic/scale descriptors and structural feel only for factory-owned frozen definitions; reuse bounded immutable keyboard voicing geometry while keeping every history-dependent ranking/line/contrary choice current; isolate musician-card and arc dependencies from 40 ms clock/meter-only snapshots. External/restored/shallow-frozen definitions continue through fresh preparation and validation. The geometry cache is bounded to 256 entries and retains no caller history. Preparation consumes no future solo evidence or generated notes. No checkpoint schema/fingerprint, note, rhythm, gate, velocity, scheduling horizon, compute budget, observation timing or late-callback safeguard is intentionally changed.

Behavioral validation: pre-change SHA256 pins cover every 0.8 returned event/trace/checkpoint for full 52-bar Blues in all four styles; each bar also matches JSON-restored continuation. Additional tests cover 104-bar loop runs with changing observations, interleaved independent performances, mutable and shallow-frozen caller data, cache eviction, malformed warm inputs, detached outputs and one-time whole-score preparation. Geometry reuse first failed red with 76 quartal builder calls for two requests, then passed with 38 for the first and none for reuse at a different requested register. New ensemble preparation tests initially failed because preparation was absent; their first green attempt exposed a test fixture using the nonexistent solo_develop phase, corrected to solo_build without changing runtime policy. Independent review found no blocker in static ownership, boundary lookup or causal continuation.

Local broad suite: `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=4 npm test -- --run` passed 8,497 plus 40 existing expected failures across 536 files in 48.26 s. Two added bounded-work assertions subsequently passed in the 14-test preparation batch (866 ms). Initial typecheck found two errors in the new geometry test fixture because its keyboard-line request omitted required style/stepSemitones fields; corrected fixture passed 5/5 locally. Sequential placeholder-env `npm run check` then passed zero errors/warnings. No application validation was removed to fix either fixture issue. Browser timing comparison and repeated no-retry full performances remain pending.

Artifacts: `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-11-10/webkit-reliability/`. Audition guidance after delivery: refresh and start new complete Mankunku Blues Piano and Organ takes, then native styles; listen for uninterrupted completion and the same 0.8 musical behavior. 0.9.0 work remains deferred until this reliability checkpoint is verified.


Further diagnosis prevents a premature success claim: with static preparation in place, a profiled WebKit run generated its first 12 bars within 5 ms but failed on an already committed downbeat callback, 81.712 ms late. Its export is retained under local sandbox `mankunku-test-10e59bb7-e3f1-4333-92d4-01281599953c`. Lower generation cost alone did not fix the issue. Extended temporary profiling measured actual audio-trigger, export-aggregation and presenter work plus timer gaps. A subsequent full take completed, but aggregating the growing committed history took up to 24 ms. Main-thread timer gaps also appeared around 30/90 s; wall-clock evidence alone does not attribute those gaps to a specific collector, browser task or OS scheduling event.

The first UI selector-only change did not work as assumed: profiling still counted 2,070 musician-card and 4,140 arc calls. Inspection of installed Svelte proxy behavior showed that replacement snapshots recreated proxies for otherwise identical nested evidence, defeating identity selectors. The component never writes nested snapshot fields, so its snapshot now uses `$state.raw` plus stable selectors. An independently reviewed full native WebKit Piano profile completed all 52 bars and reduced calls to 102/204, respectively (about 95% fewer); max measured generation 21 ms and commit 5 ms. It retained actual audio, complete dispatches and all existing musical assertions. This measurement establishes the avoided repeated work, not immunity to arbitrary main-thread suspension. No global profiling hooks are retained in the deliverable.


The aggregator now recognizes only exact renderer-owned snapshot identities through a private WeakSet. It creates fresh frozen outer arrays while sharing already-detached immutable musical descendants, avoiding full-history clone/freeze work after each bar. JSON, spread and shallow-frozen external snapshots remain untrusted and are cloned. New red/green tests assert zero owned-history clones, exact output, frozen descendant mutation rejection, detached external descendants and prior-prefix stability; 51 focused contracts passed in 2.52 s. Scheduling/publication/rollback logic is unchanged.

Final runtime broad check: `VITEST_MAX_WORKERS=4 npm test -- --run` passed 8,501 plus 40 existing expected failures across 536 files in 49.13 s. Sequential placeholder-env typecheck passed zero errors/warnings. All temporary browser profiling hooks have been removed. The final uninstrumented WebKit batch runs both native Piano/Organ full Blues cases with `--project=webkit --grep 'Swing full tune' --workers=2 --retries=0 --repeat-each=3`; the repository's WebKit project cap correctly uses one worker. Six fresh full takes must pass independently, with no retry rescue. Result: **5 passed, 1 failed** in10.9 minutes. The second Piano take stopped after 46 sampled bars with `late_callback`. The failed export and unmodified assertions are retained in sandbox `mankunku-test-852d338e-42f4-45f1-b9c1-7103f958baff`; this attempt is explicitly not a fix.


The remaining defect is dispatch ownership: a committed note still depended on a later JavaScript/Tone callback. A main-thread pause could miss that callback even when generation had finished within budget. The renderer now optionally hands an accepted bar's exact timestamps directly to native Web Audio. Musical generation, observation eligibility, the 25 ms compute limit, generation trigger and deadline checks remain unchanged. Publication and deadline rejection happen before any native source is allocated. Once the bar is committed, a failure while arming it is retained as a failed committed playback and stops the session, never pretending the bar was rolled back or retrying it. Legacy callback runtimes retain their full guards.

Owned cancellation required a separate fix: installed smplr 1.0 marks a duration-bearing voice as stopping when its future release is first scheduled, so its returned stop closure cannot shorten that release. A public BaseAudioContext facade captures only sources created synchronously inside the explicit arm scope; owned source.stop(now) supersedes the duration stop, including before onset. Each instrument has its own normal scheduler delegate; outside the arm scope legacy scheduling remains unchanged. No platform prototype, actual context or dependency source is patched. A final per-source start-time check rejects overdue starts even if the clock advances during a batch. Reentrancy and partial failures cancel only owned sources. Played receipts continue to mean submitted to the audio engine, not proven human hearing; take completion still waits for actual form end and release tail.

Independent review caught provisional-audio risk in an earlier design; arming was moved after transaction acceptance before browser validation. It also caught the need for a final clock guard at actual source.start, and shared scheduler disposal was replaced with separate per-instrument delegates. Unit tests cover accepted evidence before arming, no source allocation on rejected publication, partial failure, stale owners, reentrant stop, no retry and future cancellation. Real installed-sampler PCM counterexamples and fresh full-tune no-retry browser checks remain in progress.

Native dispatch alone is also not the complete fix: its first six-take no-retry WebKit batch passed 5 and failed 1 in 9.9 minutes. The remaining failure was generation `compute_budget` near bar 11, not a late callback (sandbox `mankunku-test-82ad3e03-106c-4852-8bf8-8063004e606a`). The unchanged 25 ms guard stays active. Further work moves known chord geometry out of playback and avoids revalidating/recloning exact privately owned immutable checkpoints, while retaining external JSON validation and replay contracts.

The new native PCM harness initially failed because numeric-string sampler buffer keys were aliases rather than MIDI 60 and produced silence; it now uses the explicit C4 key. Its standalone page also needed the fixture's hydration marker. Firefox lacks OfflineAudioContext.suspend, so only its mid-note offline case is explicitly skipped; its other native cancellation cases remain active, alongside the existing Linux Firefox real-time-audio limitation. These are fixture/API coverage limits, not evidence of a product audio fix. All failed logs remain available.

Further bounded preparation is now implemented. `prepareCoordinatedVoicings` warms legal known-chart placements for all six selectable families before playback, retaining the 256-entry cap and lazy fallback for charts exceeding the working set. Ineligible texture requests share identical full geometry; per-request traces and history-dependent ranking remain unchanged. A private WeakMap binds a produced deeply frozen checkpoint only to its exact factory-owned frozen definition. That path reuses immutable state without rehashing and cloning it again; JSON/restored, copied, shallow-frozen, forged, cross-definition and externally generated checkpoints still validate and detach. New contracts failed red before the fast path; 67 focused checkpoint/replay tests and 22 preparation/integration tests passed. Independent review found no blocking trust, mutation, causal or eviction issue.

Native PCM/cancellation and deliberate main-thread-pause tests passed **16 checks with 2 explicit Firefox skips** in 26.2 s (sandbox `mankunku-test-57925689-6c80-4e26-b138-34c741b4d892`). Both Chromium and WebKit played a full 80 ms gate and release while the page thread was blocked for 450 ms, beginning one 44.1 kHz sample (about 0.023 ms) after the requested timestamp. AudioWorklet frame evidence verifies the note began and ended during the pause; a delayed JS callback could not satisfy it. The real smplr control audibly leaked a cancelled future duration voice, while native owned cancellation produced zero PCM, cancelled partial starts and preserved the other owner's sound. This proves the dispatch/cancellation mechanism, not arbitrary-pause immunity for decisions that have not yet been generated. Repeated complete WebKit takes are rerunning with the additional preparation; final cross-browser application checks remain pending.

### 2026-11-10 — 0.9.0: keyboard suspension and resolution

Intent: continue the musicality roadmap after 0.8.1 reliability delivery, with an audible but occasional harmonic gesture from P47. Keep steady accompaniment, connected top voices and each style's native rhythm; do not add attacks or trade harmonic support for variety. Work stays on `musicality`; validation is direct local Node plus the isolated local Colima/Docker browser sandbox, without CircleCI CLI, hosted jobs or sidecars.

Implementation: the independently fingerprinted `keyboardSuspensions` policy decorates an already accepted pair of identical plain-dominant rootless chords. The first chord moves one lower/inner major third up to the fourth; the second resolves to the original third. The complete pair stays within the same harmony and bar. Both attack times, gates, velocities, note counts, upper voice and final chord remain intact. Changed contrary-motion choices, responses, guide-tone restraint, dense/active solo evidence, heads, section endings, shared arrivals and terminal arrangements retain priority. Bossa's native syncopation and Ballad's existing paired pads supply their own opportunities. Other chords and whole-bar pads remain ordinary.

The bass is generated first from the same causal history. Its actual scheduled third can veto a simultaneous suspended fourth during the keyboard gate; it is never rewritten to make the keyboard idea fit. Missing bass evidence declines the gesture. All ordinary 0.8 bass/drum events and keyboard rhythms remain exact in the tested full forms. A separate seeded draw and accepted-only three-bar cooldown avoid a fixed repeating slot. The small `lastSuspensionBar` checkpoint field is restored/validated, preserved through silence/replies/endings, and bound by the policy fingerprint. No unresolved cell crosses a scheduling transaction. The 0.8.1 native-clock renderer, deadlines, compute budget and static preparation are unchanged.

Actual voiced metrics are recalculated for the suspended chord and its resolution; pre-decoration connected/contrary comparisons are removed for this pair. Matching receipts on both emitted attacks support the new live/review card description. The page includes concise audition guidance; P47's ledger row is partial, with source/tests and explicit remaining 9–root, 13–5 and cross-bar work. No acoustic solo recognition or human acceptance is claimed.

Initial admission allowed only an inner third and a fixed seeded bar parity; the full-form contracts exposed no Ballad witness. That restriction was replaced with a lower-or-inner voice (still preserving the top line) and actual accepted-gesture cooldown. No playback guard, musical-support assertion or native rhythm was relaxed. ### 2026-11-10 — 0.11.0: phrase expression for bass, keyboard and drums

Intent: add one audible roadmap improvement per member without returning to sparse keyboard support: B49 walking backbeat emphasis, P14 short/held keyboard contrast, D37 snare ghost expression. All work stays on `musicality`; validation is direct local Node plus the isolated local Colima/Docker browser sandbox, with no CircleCI or sidecars.

Changes: selected walking phrases lighten beats 1/3 and emphasize 2/4 within velocity headroom; keyboard pairs use distinct Piano/Organ short/held gates only when they fit the current harmony and preserve total gated support; selected drum fills soften only an existing interior snare while preserving their launch and landing. Ordinary two-snare commentary can use an accented statement and softer answer. The new policy is independently fingerprinted and seeded by player/phrase. Actual event receipts feed compact factual musician cards. Protected responses, dense observations, joint endings, quarter-note pulse, keyboard suspension pairs and gain calibration remain authoritative.

Scope decisions and failures: the new pure-device contracts first failed on the missing module (`pure-red.log`), then passed after implementation. Initial full-tune tests found no qualifying native Bossa/Ballad keyboard pairs under the support-preservation rule, so the release explicitly scopes all three additions to Swing/Straight; native styles ignore the option and stay byte-identical. No support assertion was weakened to manufacture a native witness. Ordinary paired snare commentary was too rare in the seed-42 Blues, so the drum implementation also shapes the interior stroke of an already selected three-hit fill. It never inserts a fill or weakens its landing. A new assertion comparing triplet offsets by exact floating-point subtraction failed; it now uses 1e-8 beat tolerance while preserving exact event time/velocity matching. Intermediate evidence remains in `performance-first.log`, `performance-second.log`, `performance-third.log` and `opportunities.log`.

JSON continuation, declared loops, dense/future observations, actual receipt validation, all three instruments and ending protection are covered. Focused contracts passed 25/25 in 8.70 s (`contracts-final.log`); further source review adds exact matched-take footprint assertions.

### 2026-11-10 — 0.12.0: bass figures, drum catches and altered-dominant keyboard color

Intent: add one audible plan improvement per player without thinning keyboard support or making every part busier. B9 repeats a selected root on the second walking pulse; D77 gives the existing beat-three kick a modest response to that actual bass figure; P48 lowers an inner ninth to a flat ninth on a selected dominant-to-tonic cadence. Bass/drum changes are Swing/Straight only; keyboard color also uses existing Bossa/Ballad native attacks and gates. The +6 dB Master reference and mixer layout stay unchanged. Full design, exclusions and causal evidence are documented in architecture and the /musicality ledger.

Before edits, the clean 0.11 source at `5f191b6ad7d0055312ee40e9e6a1e070c8f88eb9` generated 48 full-performance hashes (four styles, Blues/AABA, seeds 42/7/19, Piano/Organ), captured in `tests/fixtures/audio/musicality-0.11-hashes.json`; `baseline.log` passed in 2.53 s. All remain exact when the new option is omitted. The new pure-device contracts failed first on the missing module (`pure-red.log`). A separate bass/harmonic-conflict contract also failed first (`bass-conflict-red.log`); it now distinguishes intentional root/flat-ninth tension from a conflicting natural-ninth bass gate, and ignores a later non-overlapping bass note.

Initial full-performance testing found bass/kick witnesses but no keyboard color (`performance-first.log`). Inspection of actual voicings/plans (`opportunities.log`) showed that the shared rhythmic admission was excluding the very section-ending cadences where this harmonic device belongs. Keyboard color now has a separate admission: it can coexist with an existing fill or bass/drum-only setup, while keyboard-involving reservations, responses, busy solo evidence and endings retain priority. No attacks or gate durations were added, and no prior support assertion was weakened. Bossa's reserved bass/drum-only setup was the final missing native opportunity (`performance-second.log`); the scoped admission produced witnesses in all four styles (`performance-third.log`). Final contracts also reject the already anticipated next-chord lookup.

Focused pure/full-performance/card contracts passed **347/347 in 9.64 s** (`contracts.log`); after explicit bass-clash coverage and exact keyboard gate/velocity assertions, the new musical contracts passed **18/18 in 8.91 s** (`contracts-final.log`). They cover actual bass/drum causality, unchanged final bass history, support/timing preservation, real voicing memory, absent-policy compatibility, JSON replay, loop seams, dense and future observations, endings and forged/missing explanation receipts. Typecheck found two seed accesses through the narrower BarInfo type and readonly mutation in the new negative-test copies; accesses now use the typed definition bars, and tests alter detached GeneratedBacking copies. Runtime seed values are unchanged.

Bass exit motion allows up to a fifth while retaining its final two approach notes. No previous support, timing or native-audio thresholds were weakened.

## 0.14.0 — arpeggios, drop-3 and held-chord cross-stick (10 November 2026)

Intent: add one audible vocabulary/interaction improvement per musician from B27, P24 and D32 while retaining the full-tune support established through 0.13. Work remains on `musicality`; no CI, CircleCI CLI, sidecar, dev/main merge or production deployment is used.

Implementation: selected Swing/Straight bass bars outline root–third–fifth before their existing fourth-beat approach, committing actual last-two-note memory. Complete enclosures/double-chromatic approaches, scalar continuations and reserved figures remain protected. Keyboard drop-3 lowers one inner voice an octave on an existing four-note chord, preserving its melody and rhythm, within MIDI 45–84, maximum 28-semitone span and at least seven semitones above overlapping bass. It commits actual voicing memory across all native styles; old policies keep the 48 floor. An existing Swing/Straight snare comment can become quieter cross-stick beneath an actual held keyboard chord, retaining original snare scheduling and limb. Native Bossa/Ballad drums and bass retain their existing character. ## 2026-11-11 — 0.15.0: opening recall, fourth-stack color and snare/kick dialogue

**Intent.** Add one audible, source-backed development per musician from B32, P25 and D47 while retaining full-tune support and native style character. Work remains on `musicality`; all validation is local Node or the dedicated local Colima/Docker browser sandbox. No CI, CircleCI CLI, hosted pipeline or sidecar is used.

**Implementation.** `phraseDialogue` independently fingerprints the cumulative 0.15 engine. Bass retains only the previous actual four-pulse opening and may recall its contour on beat two, adjusting by at most a semitone to the current scale. It keeps the entrance and final approach, protects reserved figures and cannot immediately recall a recall. Keyboard can add a five-note 3/7 shell plus a genuine 9–5–root fourth stack to a settled plain seventh chord; current attacks/gates remain and actual pitches update voice-leading and contour memory. Native Bossa/Ballad rhythms remain. Drums can move the last stroke of a complete two/three-snare figure to a quieter kick while preserving an existing middle ghost and the scheduled snare tick; nominal and scheduled foot occupancy and nearby piano onsets veto unsafe substitutions. Live cards require actual matching events, source evidence and traces. The implementation ledger and architecture describe these as partial capabilities, with human acceptance pending.

**Behavioral development and corrections.** Captured 48 complete 0.14 event/trace/checkpoint SHA-256 baselines from `b9a374e05a8dc198afdac11b49ad265fad5db103` before engine edits (`baseline.log`, 2.64 s). Initial helper tests failed before implementation (`tdd-red.log`). Full-tune probes showed literal interval transposition and two-snare-only dialogue were too restricted in the Blues. Bass now permits a one-semitone scale adjustment that preserves direction; complete three-snare fills can change their final stroke while retaining the ghosted middle. Keyboard admission accepts a full settled bar after its first beat as well as repeated harmony, and a three- or four-note source chord. Existing colors/suspensions still take priority. The first typecheck flagged a mutable cast in a temporary survey script; the diagnostic script was archived and removed. Example seed 147 is discovered from the actual engine, not hardcoded gesture insertion.

**Audition.** Refresh `/musicality`, confirm 0.15.0 and select **Load 0.15 example**, then **Listen**. It chooses Mankunku Blues in F, Swing, 140 BPM and two solo choruses, preserving your keyboard and mix without autoplay. At the default 0.62 swing setting (also checked at 0.67): first solo **bar 6**, hear the bassist reuse the previous bar's opening shape against the next harmony (absolute bar 22); first solo **bar 8**, hear the snare figure finish on kick (absolute bar 24); second solo **bar 9**, hear the more open, fourth-stacked keyboard upper color over a defining shell (absolute bar 37). Briefly isolate each musician, then restore the full band. Repeat with Organ. Use New energy arc for other performances; these landmarks are specific to the example. Bossa/Ballad gain the keyboard color within native rhythms; their bass/drum patterns are protected. Save notes on whether each gesture connects naturally, keeps support and avoids sounding imposed.

**Limits / next.** This is bounded motivic walking, a quartal upper color over a shell, and timbral dialogue on existing drum rhythms. It is not unrestricted motivic sequencing, a full modal voicing palette, physiological limb modeling or live-human listening. The source-backed audition is a listening preview. Prior 0.14's isolated concurrent WebKit compute-budget stop remains documented; this release keeps existing failure/deadline assertions and uses one worker for realtime full takes. Next work should follow listening feedback about these connections and extend accepted motifs across larger phrases only where the harmony and ensemble leave room.

Validation, final review, engine checkpoint and preview binding follow below. Artifacts: `/Users/avitus/.codex/artifacts/musicality-2026-11-11/phrase-dialogue/`.

**Native balance regression and fix.** The first 36-case real-sample matrix completed with 33 passes and three consistent seed-7 Bossa failures (one per engine): Organ was 2.02–2.04 dB below Piano against the unchanged 2 dB limit. 0.14 already sat near this boundary (Chromium -1.969 dB). The new five-note color now scales its velocity by `sqrt(originalVoiceCount / 5)` on both keyboards; this avoids purchasing color with an unintended voice-count level jump. A focused Chromium native rerun improved to -1.994 dB but left too little cross-engine margin. The shared short-gate Bossa Organ calibration therefore gains a modest 6% (+0.506 dB) in both Original/Coordinated and live/WAV rendering. User faders, mute, Piano, other styles and historical generated events remain unchanged. The calibration test failed first, then checks live bank reuse, mute and unchanged WAV event dynamics. The revised native matrix also includes seed 147 and a complete 128-bar Ballad AABA with actual fourth-stack events, since the ordinary Blues seed matrix does not exercise that color in Ballad. A separate source-memory deletion test failed before validation was tightened; restored walking checkpoints now reject missing, stale or malformed opening memory.

The expanded full Ballad AABA render exposed an additional calibration gap: Organ was **+2.238 dB** above Piano after the previous Ballad trim. It includes 128 bars and actual fourth-stack events. The shared Ballad Organ factor changes from 0.9 to 0.86 (**-0.395 dB**), with existing live/bounce/mute assertions retained. This is a shared output calibration refinement, not a change to Original note generation. The native balance ceiling remains 2 dB; affected Ballad cases and any interrupted CDN cases are rerun without altering their musical assertions.

The native matrix retains seed 147 and additionally renders the final Swing example.

The corrected cards/example tests pass (`cards-corrected.log`); generated audio is unchanged.
